Yim — Minecraft · MIT App Inventor · Micro:bit

265 sessions · Minecraft Education, MIT App Inventor & Micro:bit · Tue / Sat / Sun · 90 minutes each · 17 Nov 2026 – 21 Nov 2027 · 397.5 hours total.

Please confirm: 265 matches Yim’s CIY points total, and his ledger showed 34 sessions remaining — the 265-session count is still to confirm with Rica. Block-based / visual only, no text programming. Topics only: Minecraft Education, MIT App Inventor and Micro:bit.

Showing 265 of 265 sessions

Session 1Plan village on paper, build house 1Tuesday 17 Nov 2026
Tuesday 17 Nov 2026 | M1 Village & Farm

Date: Tuesday 17 Nov 2026, 15:30–17:00 (90 min) | Unit: M1 Village & Farm | Topic: Plan village on paper, build house 1

Learning objective: By the end of this session, Yim can plan village on paper, build house 1 — working, tested, and shown to Rica.

Materials: Minecraft Education, laptop/computer, mouse, paper + pencil for village plan, timer, screenshot tool. World: new flat / village world, saved as Village-Farm.

Lesson plan — 90 minutes:

  • 0–10 min — Welcome & plan: Recap last session in Yim's words. Show today's goal in one sentence: "Plan village on paper, build house 1." Plan on paper first — draw / list the 3 steps before touching the computer.
  • 10–20 min — Demo: Rica shows the key blocks / build for "Plan village on paper, build house 1" step by step, in Minecraft Education. Yim repeats the demo once, with Rica watching.
  • 20–65 min — Build: Yim builds "Plan village on paper, build house 1". One main task only. Timer visible. Rica helps only if he is stuck for 3 minutes. Keep the build small enough to finish today.
  • 65–80 min — Test & fix: Walk through / play the build in Minecraft. Find one thing to fix. Fix it. Save the world and take a screenshot of the working version.
  • 80–90 min — Share & close: Yim shows what he made, says what worked and what to improve. Rica writes the report note from this. Agree the homework below.

Success check: The plan village on paper, build house 1 works when tested, Yim can explain the 3 steps he used, and there is a saved screenshot / project to show.

Homework: Finish the paper plan — draw / list the next 3 steps for the next session.

Saturday 21 Nov 2026 | A1 First Apps

Tuesday 05 Jan 2027 | M2 Redstone Machines

Date: Tuesday 05 Jan 2027, 15:30–17:00 (90 min) | Unit: M2 Redstone Machines | Topic: Redstone basics - power + torch

Learning objective: By the end of this session, Yim can redstone basics - power + torch — working, tested, and shown to Rica.

Materials: Minecraft Education, laptop/computer, paper + pencil for circuit sketch, timer, screenshot tool. World: Redstone test world with redstone dust, torches, levers, pressure plates, pistons, lamps.

Lesson plan — 90 minutes:

  • 0–10 min — Welcome & plan: Recap last session in Yim's words. Show today's goal in one sentence: "Redstone basics - power + torch." Plan on paper first — draw / list the 3 steps before touching the computer.
  • 10–20 min — Demo: Rica shows the key blocks / build for "Redstone basics - power + torch" step by step, in Minecraft Education. Yim repeats the demo once, with Rica watching.
  • 20–65 min — Build: Yim builds "Redstone basics - power + torch". One main task only. Timer visible. Rica helps only if he is stuck for 3 minutes. Keep the build small enough to finish today.
  • 65–80 min — Test & fix: Walk through / play the build in Minecraft. Find one thing to fix. Fix it. Save the world and take a screenshot of the working version.
  • 80–90 min — Share & close: Yim shows what he made, says what worked and what to improve. Rica writes the report note from this. Agree the homework below.

Success check: The redstone basics - power + torch works when tested, Yim can explain the 3 steps he used, and there is a saved screenshot / project to show.

Homework: One small task only: add one small improvement to "Redstone basics - power + torch" — one room / button / pattern — and bring it to the next session.

Date: Sunday 09 May 2027, 13:30–15:00 (90 min) | Unit: A3 Data, Lists & Screens | Topic: Review Data unit

Learning objective: By the end of this session, Yim can review data unit — working, tested, and shown to Rica.

Materials: Computer with MIT App Inventor, phone/tablet with Companion app, internet, paper + pencil for screen map, timer, list of quiz / collection items.

Lesson plan — 90 minutes:

  • 0–10 min — Welcome & plan: Recap last session in Yim's words. Show today's goal in one sentence: "Review Data unit." Plan on paper first — draw / list the 3 steps before touching the computer.
  • 10–20 min — Demo: Rica shows the Designer + Blocks for "Review Data unit" step by step. Yim repeats the demo once, with Rica watching.
  • 20–65 min — Build: Yim builds "Review Data unit" in MIT App Inventor. One main task only. Timer visible. Rica helps only if he is stuck for 3 minutes. Test with the Companion app as he goes.
  • 65–80 min — Test & fix: Run the app on the phone/tablet with the Companion app. Find one thing to fix. Fix it. Save the project and take a screenshot of the working app.
  • 80–90 min — Share & close: Yim shows what he made, says what worked and what to improve. Rica writes the report note from this. Agree the homework below.

Success check: The review data unit works when tested, Yim can explain the 3 steps he used, and there is a saved screenshot / project to show.

Homework: Show the finished work to a parent / family member and tell them the 3 steps you used. No new build.

Tuesday 29 Jun 2027 | M5 Coding Blocks & Automation

Date: Tuesday 29 Jun 2027, 15:30–17:00 (90 min) | Unit: M5 Coding Blocks & Automation | Topic: Coding blocks - loops

Learning objective: By the end of this session, Yim can coding blocks - loops — working, tested, and shown to Rica.

Materials: Minecraft Education (Code Builder / coding blocks), laptop/computer, paper + pencil for flow sketch, timer, screenshot tool. World: automation test world.

Lesson plan — 90 minutes:

  • 0–10 min — Welcome & plan: Recap last session in Yim's words. Show today's goal in one sentence: "Coding blocks - loops." Plan on paper first — draw / list the 3 steps before touching the computer.
  • 10–20 min — Demo: Rica shows the key blocks / build for "Coding blocks - loops" step by step, in Minecraft Education. Yim repeats the demo once, with Rica watching.
  • 20–65 min — Build: Yim builds "Coding blocks - loops". One main task only. Timer visible. Rica helps only if he is stuck for 3 minutes. Keep the build small enough to finish today.
  • 65–80 min — Test & fix: Walk through / play the build in Minecraft. Find one thing to fix. Fix it. Save the world and take a screenshot of the working version.
  • 80–90 min — Share & close: Yim shows what he made, says what worked and what to improve. Rica writes the report note from this. Agree the homework below.

Success check: The coding blocks - loops works when tested, Yim can explain the 3 steps he used, and there is a saved screenshot / project to show.

Homework: One small task only: add one small improvement to "Coding blocks - loops" — one room / button / pattern — and bring it to the next session.

Date: Sunday 25 Jul 2027, 11:00–12:30 (90 min) | Unit: M5 Coding Blocks & Automation | Topic: Test farm

Learning objective: By the end of this session, Yim can test farm — working, tested, and shown to Rica.

Materials: Minecraft Education (Code Builder / coding blocks), laptop/computer, paper + pencil for flow sketch, timer, screenshot tool. World: automation test world.

Lesson plan — 90 minutes:

  • 0–10 min — Welcome & plan: Recap last session in Yim's words. Show today's goal in one sentence: "Test farm." Plan on paper first — draw / list the 3 steps before touching the computer.
  • 10–20 min — Demo: Rica shows the key blocks / build for "Test farm" step by step, in Minecraft Education. Yim repeats the demo once, with Rica watching.
  • 20–65 min — Build: Yim builds "Test farm". One main task only. Timer visible. Rica helps only if he is stuck for 3 minutes. Keep the build small enough to finish today.
  • 65–80 min — Test & fix: Walk through / play the build in Minecraft. Find one thing to fix. Fix it. Save the world and take a screenshot of the working version.
  • 80–90 min — Share & close: Yim shows what he made, says what worked and what to improve. Rica writes the report note from this. Agree the homework below.

Success check: The test farm works when tested, Yim can explain the 3 steps he used, and there is a saved screenshot / project to show.

Homework: One small task only: add one small improvement to "Test farm" — one room / button / pattern — and bring it to the next session.
